{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui.worktrees/impl-295/crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "    fn on_props_changed(old: &Props, new: &Props) -> Vec<Event> {\n        assert_eq!(\n            old.id, new.id,\n            \"signature_pad::Props.id must remain stable after init\"\n        );\n\n        Vec::new()\n    }\n\n    fn transition(\n        state: &Self::State,\n        event: &Self::Event,\n        ctx: &Self::Context,\n        _props: &Self::Props,\n    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {\n        // Focus/Blur always pass through regardless of disabled/readonly.\n        match event {\n            Event::Focus => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    ctx.focused = true;\n                }));\n            }\n            Event::Blur => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    ctx.focused = false;\n                }));\n            }",
  "new_string": "    fn on_props_changed(old: &Props, new: &Props) -> Vec<Event> {\n        assert_eq!(\n            old.id, new.id,\n            \"signature_pad::Props.id must remain stable after init\"\n        );\n\n        let mut events = Vec::new();\n        if old.data != new.data {\n            events.push(Event::SyncData);\n        }\n        if old.disabled != new.disabled\n            || old.readonly != new.readonly\n            || old.pen_color != new.pen_color\n            || old.pen_width != new.pen_width\n            || old.min_distance != new.min_distance\n        {\n            events.push(Event::SyncProps);\n        }\n        events\n    }\n\n    fn transition(\n        state: &Self::State,\n        event: &Self::Event,\n        ctx: &Self::Context,\n        props: &Self::Props,\n    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {\n        // Focus/Blur and the prop-sync events always pass through, regardless of\n        // disabled/read-only (prop sync must be able to re-enable the pad).\n        match event {\n            Event::Focus => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    ctx.focused = true;\n                }));\n            }\n            Event::Blur => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    ctx.focused = false;\n                }));\n            }\n            Event::SyncProps => {\n                let disabled = props.disabled;\n                let readonly = props.readonly;\n                let pen_color = props.pen_color.clone();\n                let pen_width = props.pen_width;\n                let min_distance = props.min_distance;\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(move |ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    ctx.disabled = disabled;\n                    ctx.readonly = readonly;\n                    ctx.pen_color = pen_color;\n                    ctx.pen_width = pen_width;\n                    ctx.min_distance = min_distance;\n                }));\n            }\n            Event::SyncData => {\n                let new_data = props.data.clone();\n                // Reconcile the displayed state with the new controlled data,\n                // but never reorder an in-flight stroke. Switching to\n                // uncontrolled mode (`None`) keeps the existing local value.\n                let plan = match (&new_data, matches!(state, State::Drawing)) {\n                    (_, true) | (None, _) => TransitionPlan::new(),\n                    (Some(data), false) => TransitionPlan::to(if data.is_empty() {\n                        State::Idle\n                    } else {\n                        State::Completed\n                    }),\n                };\n                return Some(plan.apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    if let Some(data) = new_data {\n                        ctx.data.set(data.clone());\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(Some(data));\n                    } else {\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(None);\n                    }\n                }));\n            }"
}

fresh_service(test_props());\n        drop(service.set_props(\n            test_props()\n                .disabled(true)\n                .readonly(true)\n                .pen_color(\"#abcdef\")\n assert_eq!(service.context().pen_color, \"#abcdef\");\n        assert_eq!(service.context().pen_width, 7.0);\n        assert_eq!(service.context().min_distance, 9.0);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn set_props_can_reenable_a_disabled_pad() {\n        let mut service = fresh_service(test_props().disabled(true));\n        assert!(service.context().disabled);\n        drop(service.set_props(test_props().disabled(false)));\n        assert!(!service.context().disabled);\n        // And drawing works again afterwards.\n        drop(service.send(Event::DrawStart {\n            x: 0.0,\n            y: 0.0,\n            pressure: 0.5,\n        }));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Drawing);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn set_props_syncs_controlled_data_and_reconciles_state() {\n        let mut service = fresh_service(test_props().data(SignatureData::default()));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Idle);\n\n        // Parent pushes a non-empty controlled signature: state becomes Completed.\n        drop(service.set_props(test_props().data(data_with(2))));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Completed);\n        assert_eq!(service.context().data.get().strokes.len(), 2);\n\n        // Parent clears the controlled signature: state returns to Idle.\n        drop(service.set_props(test_props().data(SignatureData::default())));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Idle);\n        assert!(service.context().data.get().is_empty());\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn set_props_does_not_reorder_state_mid_stroke() {\n        let mut service = fresh_service(test_props().data(SignatureData::default()));\n        drop(service.send(Event::DrawStart {\n            x: 0.0,\n            y: 0.0,\n            pressure: 0.5,\n        }));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Drawing);\n        // A controlled-data sync arriving mid-stroke updates data but keeps the\n        // active drawing state.\n        drop(service.set_props(test_props().data(data_with(1))));\n        assert_eq!(service.state(), &State::Drawing);\n    }\n\n    //   "new_string": "    fn on_props_changed(old: &Self::Props, new: &Self::Props) -> Vec<Self::Event> {\n        assert_eq!(old.id, new.id, \"signature_pad::Props.id must remain stable after init\");\n\n        let mut events = Vec::new();\n        if old.data != new.data {\n            events.push(Event::SyncData);\n        }\n        if old.disabled != new.disabled\n            || old.readonly != new.readonly\n            || old.pen_color != new.pen_color\n            || old.pen_width != new.pen_width\n            || old.min_distance != new.min_distance\n        {\n            events.push(Event::SyncProps);\n        }\n        events\n    }\n\n    fn transition(\n        state: &Self::State,\n        event: &Self::Event,\n        ctx: &Self::Context,\n        props: &Self::Props,\n    ) -> Option<TransitionPlan<Self>> {\n        // Focus/Blur and the prop-sync events always pass through, regardless of\n        // disabled/read-only (prop sync must be able to re-enable the pad).\n        match event {\n            Event::Focus => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx| ctx.focused = true));\n            }\n            Event::Blur => {\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(|ctx| ctx.focused = false));\n            }\n            Event::SyncProps => {\n                let disabled = props.disabled;\n                let readonly = props.readonly;\n                let pen_color = props.pen_color.clone();\n                let pen_width = props.pen_width;\n                let min_distance = props.min_distance;\n                return Some(TransitionPlan::context_only(move |ctx| {\n                    ctx.disabled = disabled;\n                    ctx.readonly = readonly;\n                    ctx.pen_color = pen_color;\n                    ctx.pen_width = pen_width;\n                    ctx.min_distance = min_distance;\n                }));\n            }\n            Event::SyncData => {\n                let new_data = props.data.clone();\n                // Reconcile the displayed state with the new controlled data, but\n                // never reorder an in-flight stroke. Switching to uncontrolled\n                // mode (`None`) keeps the existing local value.\n                let plan = match (&new_data, matches!(state, State::Drawing)) {\n                    (_, true) | (None, _) => TransitionPlan::new(),\n                    (Some(data), false) => TransitionPlan::to(if data.is_empty() {\n                        State::Idle\n                    } else {\n                        State::Completed\n                    }),\n                };\n                return Some(plan.apply(move |ctx| {\n                    if let Some(data) = new_data {\n                        ctx.data.set(data.clone());\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(Some(data));\n                    } else {\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(None);\n                    }\n                }));\n            }\n            _ => {}\n        }\n\n        if ctx.disabled || ctx.readonly {\n            return None;\n        }"
}

Switching to\n                // uncontrolled mode (`None`) keeps the existing local value.\n                let plan = match (&new_data, matches!(state, State::Drawing)) {\n                    (_, true) | (None, _) => TransitionPlan::new(),\n                    (Some(data), false) => TransitionPlan::to(if data.is_empty() {\n                        State::Idle\n                    } else {\n                        State::Completed\n                    }),\n                };\n                return Some(plan.apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    if let Some(data) = new_data {\n                        ctx.data.set(data.clone());\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(Some(data));\n                    } else {\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(None);\n                    }\n                }));\n            }",
  "new_string": "            Event::SyncData => {\n                let new_data = props.data.clone();\n                // Emptiness after the sync: the new controlled value, or — when\n                // dropping to uncontrolled — the retained local value.\n                let empty_after = match &new_data {\n                    Some(data) => data.is_empty(),\n                    None => ctx.data.pending().is_empty(),\n                };\n                // Reconcile the displayed state with the synced data, but never\n                // reorder an in-flight stroke.\n                let plan = if matches!(state, State::Drawing) {\n                    TransitionPlan::new()\n                } else {\n                    TransitionPlan::to(if empty_after {\n                        State::Idle\n                    } else {\n                        State::Completed\n                    })\n                };\n                return Some(plan.apply(move |ctx: &mut Context| {\n                    if let Some(data) = new_data {\n                        ctx.data.set(data.clone());\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(Some(data));\n                    } else {\n                        ctx.data.sync_controlled(None);\n                    }\n                }));\n            }"
}

> AGENT

Implementation and audit are complete. Here is the consolidated summary for your review.

## Task #295 — SignaturePad agnostic core: ready for review

### What was built
A new framework-agnostic stateful component at [crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs](crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs) (untracked) — `Idle`/`Drawing`/`Completed` state machine, full stroke data model (`SignaturePoint`/`SignatureStroke`/`SignatureData` with `to_svg_path`/`point_count`/`export`), `SignatureFormat`/`SignatureExport`, `Props`+builder, `Messages`, a typed `Effect` enum, 7-part anatomy, and the `Api`/`ConnectApi` surface. **64 unit+snapshot tests** (17 committed `.snap` fixtures), a proptest, and a spec-conformance anatomy test.

### Files changed
- **New:** `specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs` + 17 snapshots
- `specialized/mod.rs` (module wiring)
- `tests/spec_conformance/specialized.rs` (+anatomy test)
- `tests/proptest_state_machines/specialized.rs` (+invariant proptest)
- `spec/components/specialized/signature-pad.md` (spec sync)

### Audit findings — all landed in this PR (no deferral)

**Spec was non-compiling drift against the engine** (the issue's "no spec change required" predated the typed-`Effect` convention). Resolved per your "typed enum + sync spec" choice:
- Added `type Effect = Effect` (`DrawingListeners`/`AnnounceProvided`/`AnnounceCleared` markers); removed the adapter-only `use_platform_effects()` calls from agnostic effect closures. Also fixed spec-only bugs surfaced en route: `Messages` missing `PartialEq` (yet used in a `PartialEq` `Context`), `push_str(format!())` missing `&`, `connect` missing `<'a>`, `Disabled` as `set("true")` → `set_bool`.

**Two silent contract violations (both spec and impl were wrong vs. the `State` enum's own prose), fixed directly since the `State` docs dictate the answer:**
- **H1:** `Undo` removing the last stroke left `Completed` + empty data → now returns to `Idle`.
- **H2:** `DrawEnd` on a discarded tap (<2 points) entered `Completed` + empty + a spurious "Signature provided" announcement → now returns to `Idle` when the canvas stays blank, announcing only on a real commit. This also reconciles the issue's literal *"Drawing->Idle on pointer up"* test wording with the spec.

**Phase 2 (unanimous-precedent gap):** the controlled `data` prop never synced after init and runtime `disabled`/`readonly`/pen changes were ignored — all five sibling specs include `on_props_changed`; SignaturePad's was the lone omission. Added `SyncData`/`SyncProps` with state reconciliation that preserves the H1/H2 invariant (including the controlled→uncontrolled edge).

**Phase 3 (coverage):** 97.5% → **99.71% line / 99.76% region**. The single remaining uncovered line is a genuinely-unreachable defensive guard in `DrawMove` (in `Drawing`, `current_stroke` is always populated) — left per the skill.

### Verification (all green)
`cargo test` (64 unit + conformance + doctest + proptest) · `cargo clippy -p ars-components --all-targets -D warnings` · `cargo doc` (no new warnings) · `cargo xtask spec validate` + `compile-snippets` · `cargo insta test --features i18n --unreferenced=reject` (no orphans).

Not yet run (reserved for post-approval per the workflow): full `cargo xci-fast`, commit, PR.

  "old_string": "    /// Export the signature in the requested format.\n    ///\n    /// For [`SignatureFormat::Svg`] the core generates the markup from stroke\n    /// data; for [`SignatureFormat::Points`] the raw [`SignaturePoint`] vectors\n    /// are returned directly, enabling server-side vector reconstruction\n    /// regardless of display resolution.\n    ///\n    /// [`SignatureFormat::Png`], [`SignatureFormat::Jpeg`], and\n    /// [`SignatureFormat::Base64Png`] require an off-screen canvas and are\n    /// rendered by the adapter layer; calling `export` with those formats on the\n    /// agnostic core panics. Adapters render strokes onto a canvas and encode the\n    /// result rather than calling this method for raster output.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n\n            SignatureFormat::Points => SignatureExport::Points(\n                self.strokes\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|stroke| stroke.points.clone())\n                    .collect(),\n            ),\n\n            // Png, Jpeg, Base64Png require adapter-side canvas rendering.\n            SignatureFormat::Png | SignatureFormat::Jpeg | SignatureFormat::Base64Png => {\n                unimplemented!(\"Raster export is handled by the adapter layer\")\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Export format for the signature.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SignatureFormat {\n    /// PNG format.\n    Png,\n    /// SVG format.\n    Svg,\n    /// JPEG format.\n    Jpeg,\n    /// Base64-encoded PNG format.\n    Base64Png,\n    /// Raw point data.\n    Points,\n}\n\n/// Exported signature data in the requested format.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub enum SignatureExport {\n    /// PNG image bytes.\n    Png(Vec<u8>),\n    /// SVG markup string.\n    Svg(String),\n    /// JPEG image bytes.\n    Jpeg(Vec<u8>),\n    /// Base64-encoded PNG string (suitable for `data:` URIs).\n    Base64(String),\n    /// Raw point data for vector reconstruction.\n    /// Each inner `Vec` is one continuous stroke.\n    Points(Vec<Vec<SignaturePoint>>),\n}",
  "new_string": "    /// Export the signature in one of the resolution-independent formats the\n    /// agnostic core can produce.\n    ///\n    /// For [`SignatureFormat::Svg`] the core generates the markup from stroke\n    /// data; for [`SignatureFormat::Points`] the raw [`SignaturePoint`] vectors\n    /// are returned directly, enabling server-side vector reconstruction\n    /// regardless of display resolution.\n    ///\n    /// Raster formats (PNG/JPEG/base64-PNG) require an off-screen canvas and are\n    /// therefore *not* part of this enum: framework adapters expose their own\n    /// superset `SignatureFormat`/`SignatureExport` for those, rendering strokes\n    /// onto a canvas and delegating the vector formats here.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n            SignatureFormat::Points => SignatureExport::Points(\n                self.strokes\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|stroke| stroke.points.clone())\n                    .collect(),\n            ),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\n/// Resolution-independent export formats the agnostic core can produce.\n///\n/// Raster formats (PNG/JPEG/base64-PNG) need a live canvas and live in the\n/// adapter layer's own `SignatureFormat`, not here — keeping this enum free of\n/// variants the core cannot fulfil (make-invalid-states-unrepresentable).\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum SignatureFormat {\n    /// SVG path markup.\n    Svg,\n    /// Raw point data.\n    Points,\n}\n\n/// Exported signature data in one of the [`SignatureFormat`] variants.\n///\n/// Mirrors [`SignatureFormat`]: every variant here is something the core can\n/// actually return, so [`SignatureData::export`] is total. Adapters define their\n/// own export type for raster output.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub enum SignatureExport {\n    /// SVG markup string.\n    Svg(String),\n    /// Raw point data for vector reconstruction.\n    /// Each inner `Vec` is one continuous stroke.\n    Points(Vec<Vec<SignaturePoint>>),\n}"
}

**Core** ([signature_pad/mod.rs](crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs)):

> AGENT

Good question. Short answer: it's technically possible, but a feature-flagged software rasterizer baked into the agnostic core is the *weakest* of the available options. Here's the reasoning.

## What raster export actually requires

Two pure-computation steps, neither of which strictly needs a DOM:
1. **Rasterize** vector strokes → pixel buffer (variable pen width, pressure, line joins, smoothing, antialiasing, DPR).
2. **Encode** pixel buffer → PNG/JPEG bytes.

So yes — you *could* do it agnostically with `tiny-skia`/`raqote` + `png`/`image`/`jpeg-encoder`, no canvas involved.

## Why I'd still keep it out of the core

- **Heavy, non-`no_std` deps.** `ars-components` is `no_std`+alloc. A rasterizer and image encoders are large and `std`-bound. Even feature-gated, that's a big new dependency surface that needs your explicit approval per CLAUDE.md.
- **Output divergence.** The browser already rendered the signature on a `<canvas>`. `canvas.toDataURL()`/`toBlob()` exports *exactly what's on screen*, GPU-accelerated, zero extra bytes. A separate WASM rasterizer would produce subtly different output (AA, joins, pressure curve) than what the user saw — two "the PNG"s that disagree is a footgun.
- **Bundle bloat** in the browser, for a path the browser doesn't need.
- **Architectural fit.** This whole library is agnostic-core + adapters-fulfill-platform-ops (`track_pointer_drag`, `announce`, measurement via `PlatformEffects`). Canvas rasterization is the same class of thing — a platform capability, not core logic. `qr_code` sets the precedent: the core computes the *matrix*; rendering pixels is the adapter's job. SignaturePad computing *points* and letting the adapter render is the consistent shape.
- **Feature-gated enum variants are a smell.** `#[cfg(feature = "raster")]` on `SignatureFormat::Png` makes the enum's shape depend on cfg, so `export` becomes partial-by-cfg and downstream matches need `#[cfg]` too — you'd lose the MISU cleanliness we just gained.

## If cross-platform raster is genuinely wanted later

The right design isn't a feature flag — it's **injection** (dependency-inversion), keeping the core dep-free and still total:

```rust,ignore
// core defines the contract; it never pulls a rasterizer
trait SignatureRasterizer {
    fn rasterize(&self, data: &SignatureData, spec: &RenderSpec) -> Vec<u8>;
}
// export(Png) requires you to supply one -> can't ask for PNG without a renderer (MISU preserved)
```

- Browser adapter implements it via canvas (best quality, no deps).
- A separate `ars-signature-raster` crate (or a `raster` feature on a *server* crate) implements it via `tiny-skia` for headless/SSR/native.

That gives "one PNG call works everywhere" without bloating the core, without `no_std` breakage, and without divergent output paths hidden behind a flag.

## Recommendation

For **#295** (scope: agnostic core, unit tier, 5 pts, no new deps): keep the current MISU split — `Svg`/`Points` in core, raster owned by adapters. If/when a non-browser PNG path is actually needed, do it as an injected `SignatureRasterizer` capability (its own crate/feature), not feature-gated variants in `ars-components`. Want me to capture that "future raster via injected rasterizer" note in the spec so the decision isn't lost, or leave it for when the need arises?

> AGENT

All the facts line up, and they confirm your instinct is workable — with one important correction driven by the layering.

## The layering reality (and the one correction)

- `ars-core` is the base. `ars-components` and `ars-dom` are **siblings** — neither depends on the other.
- There's an exact precedent for what you want: `ars_core::PlatformEffects` (trait) is implemented by `ars_dom::WebPlatformEffects` behind the `web` feature, with `NullPlatformEffects` for SSR/tests, injected via `ArsProvider`.

The correction: **the trait can't take `SignatureData`** — `ars-dom` can't see `ars-components` types. So the contract must be **neutral**, defined in `ars-core`. The natural neutral input is the SVG path string (`to_svg_path()`) + a `RasterSpec`. Canvas renders that directly via `Path2D::new_with_path_string(...)`.

## Proposed shape (mirrors PlatformEffects exactly)

- **`ars-core`**: `trait SignatureRasterizer { fn rasterize(&self, svg_path: &str, spec: &RasterSpec) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError>; }` + `RasterSpec { width, height, pen_color, pen_width, background, format }` + `NullSignatureRasterizer` (SSR/tests).
- **`ars-components`**: keep `export()` total for `Svg`/`Points` (MISU stays intact); add `SignatureData::export_raster(&self, rasterizer: &dyn SignatureRasterizer, spec: &RasterSpec)` that feeds `self.to_svg_path()` in. You still can't get a PNG without supplying a rasterizer — MISU preserved, no panic, no feature-gated variants.
- **`ars-dom`** (`web` feature): `WebSignatureRasterizer` → offscreen `HtmlCanvasElement` + 2D context + `Path2D` + `toDataURL`/`toBlob`. Needs adding `HtmlCanvasElement`/`CanvasRenderingContext2d`/`Path2d`/`Blob` to ars-dom's web-sys feature list (new *features* of an existing dep, not a new crate) + wasm-bindgen-test browser tests.

## Two real decisions before I touch code

One caveat to flag: rendering from the SVG path gives **constant pen width** — `to_svg_path()` discards per-point pressure. Pressure-variable width would need a richer neutral input (a `Vec<Vec<(x,y,pressure)>>` in ars-core), which is more work.

And on scope: #295's issue explicitly scopes rasterization *out* ("export rasterization belong in adapters"), and this spans three crates + wasm tests + web-sys feature changes.

1	//! Platform-agnostic interface for side effects triggered by state machine transitions.
2	//!
3	//! Effect closures must be platform-agnostic — they MUST NOT call DOM APIs directly.
4	//! Instead, all platform-specific operations (focus, timers, announcements, positioning,
5	//! scroll lock) go through the [`PlatformEffects`] trait, resolved from the adapter's
6	//! framework context.
7	//!
8	//! Each adapter provides an implementation:
9	//! - `ars-dom` provides `WebPlatformEffects` (web targets via `web_sys`)
10	//! - Native adapters provide their own (e.g., AccessKit for accessibility)
11	//!
12	//! Two built-in implementations are provided:
13	//! - [`NullPlatformEffects`] — no-op for unit tests and SSR (no warnings)
14	//! - [`MissingProviderEffects`] — no-op fallback with debug-mode warnings when no
15	//!   `ArsProvider` is found in the component tree
16	
17	use alloc::{boxed::Box, string::String, vec::Vec};
18	
19	use ars_i18n::ResolvedDirection;
20	
21	/// Platform-agnostic interface for side effects triggered by [`PendingEffect`](crate::PendingEffect) closures.
22	///
23	/// Each adapter provides an implementation:
24	/// - `ars-dom` provides `WebPlatformEffects` (web targets via `web_sys`)
25	/// - Native adapters provide their own (e.g., AccessKit for accessibility)
26	///
27	/// Components resolve this via `use_platform_effects()` inside effect closures.
28	///
29	/// Requires `Send + Sync` so implementations can be wrapped in
30	/// [`Arc`](alloc::sync::Arc) and safely shared across threads on native targets.
31	/// On wasm (single-threaded), `Send + Sync` is trivially satisfied.
32	pub trait PlatformEffects: Send + Sync {
33	    // -- Focus ---------------------------------------------------------------
34	
35	    /// Focus the element with the given ID. No-op if not found.
36	    fn focus_element_by_id(&self, id: &str);
37	
38	    /// Focus the first tabbable element inside a container. No-op if not found.
39	    fn focus_first_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str);
40	
41	    /// Focus the last tabbable element inside a container. No-op if not found.
42	    fn focus_last_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str);
43	
44	    /// Return IDs of all tabbable elements inside a container in sequential tab order.
45	    ///
46	    /// Positive `tabindex` values sort before the natural tab sequence, with DOM
47	    /// order breaking ties.
48	    fn tabbable_element_ids(&self, container_id: &str) -> Vec<String>;
49	
50	    /// Focus the document body (last-resort fallback).
51	    fn focus_body(&self);
52	
53	    // -- Timers --------------------------------------------------------------
54	
55	    /// Schedule a callback after `delay_ms` milliseconds. Returns a handle for cancellation.
56	    fn set_timeout(&self, delay_ms: u32, callback: Box<dyn FnOnce()>) -> TimerHandle;
57	
58	    /// Cancel a previously scheduled timeout.
59	    fn clear_timeout(&self, handle: TimerHandle);
60	
61	    // -- Announcements -------------------------------------------------------
62	
63	    /// Announce a message to assistive technology with polite priority.
64	    fn announce(&self, message: &str);
65	
66	    /// Announce a message with assertive priority (interrupts current speech).
67	    fn announce_assertive(&self, message: &str);
68	
69	    // -- Positioning ---------------------------------------------------------
70	
```

=== ars-dom/src/platform.rs head (WebPlatformEffects struct + how it accesses web_sys) ===
//! Browser-backed [`ars_core::PlatformEffects`] implementation.

use std::{string::String, vec::Vec};

use ars_core::{PlatformEffects, Rect, TimerHandle};
use ars_i18n::ResolvedDirection;
#[cfg(all(feature = "web", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
use {
    js_sys::{Function, Reflect},
    std::{cell::RefCell, mem, rc::Rc},
    wasm_bindgen::{JsCast, closure::Closure},
    web_sys::{
        AddEventListenerOptions, Document, Element, Event, EventTarget, HtmlElement, KeyboardEvent,
        Window,
    },
};

use crate::{announcer, focus, portal, scroll_lock};

/// Production [`PlatformEffects`] implementation for browser builds.
#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy)]
pub struct WebPlatformEffects;

impl PlatformEffects for WebPlatformEffects {
    fn focus_element_by_id(&self, id: &str) {
        focus::focus_element_by_id(id);
    }

    fn focus_first_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str) {
        focus::focus_first_tabbable(container_id);
    }

    fn focus_last_tabbable(&self, container_id: &str) {
        focus::focus_last_tabbable_for_platform(container_id);
    }

    fn tabbable_element_ids(&self, container_id: &str) -> Vec<String> {
        focus::tabbable_element_ids_for_platform(container_id)
    }

=== a wasm test header (how configured) ===
//! Compatibility tests for DOM-facing z-index re-exports.

use std::sync::{Mutex, MutexGuard};

static TEST_SERIAL: Mutex<()> = Mutex::new(());

fn serial_reset() -> MutexGuard<'static, ()> {
    let guard = TEST_SERIAL
        .lock()
        .unwrap_or_else(std::sync::PoisonError::into_inner);

    ars_dom::reset_z_index(ars_dom::Z_INDEX_BASE);

    guard
}

#[test]
fn z_index_ars_dom_and_ars_core_free_functions_interleave_on_same_sequence() {
    let _guard = serial_reset();

    assert_eq!(ars_dom::next_z_index(), ars_dom::Z_INDEX_BASE);
    assert_eq!(ars_core::next_z_index(), ars_dom::Z_INDEX_BASE + 1);
    assert_eq!(ars_dom::next_z_index(), ars_dom::Z_INDEX_BASE + 2);
}

=== _impl dual-cfg pattern ===
fn focus_element_by_id_impl(id: &str) -> bool {
    get_html_element_by_id(id)
        .filter(is_focusable_element)
        .map(|element| {
            focus_element(&element, false);
        })
        .is_some()
}

#[cfg(any(not(feature = "web"), not(target_arch = "wasm32")))]
fn focus_element_by_id_impl(_id: &str) -> bool {
    false
}

#[cfg(all(feature = "web", target_arch = "wasm32"))]
fn focus_first_tabbable_impl(container_id: &str) -> bool {
    let Some(container) = get_element_by_id(container_id) else {
        return false;
    };

    let Some(first) = get_tabbable_elements(&container).into_iter().next() else {
        return false;
    };

  "content": "//! Platform-agnostic interface for rasterizing vector strokes into an encoded\n//! image (PNG/JPEG).\n//!\n//! The agnostic core models a signature (and similar freehand input) as vector\n//! strokes. Turning those into a raster image requires a pixel surface and an\n//! encoder, which only the platform provides — so, like\n//! [`PlatformEffects`](crate::PlatformEffects), rasterization goes through a\n//! trait resolved from the adapter layer:\n//!\n//! - `ars-dom` provides `WebSignatureRasterizer` (browser `<canvas>`).\n//! - [`NullSignatureRasterizer`] is the no-op for SSR and unit tests.\n//!\n//! The contract is deliberately neutral — it takes pressure-weighted points and\n//! a [`RasterSpec`], never any component-specific type — so the lower-level\n//! `ars-dom` crate can implement it without depending on the component crates.\n\nuse alloc::{string::String, vec::Vec};\nuse core::fmt;\n\n/// A sampled stroke point for rasterization: position plus normalized pressure.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub struct RasterPoint {\n    /// The x coordinate of the point, in canvas pixels.\n    pub x: f64,\n    /// The y coordinate of the point, in canvas pixels.\n    pub y: f64,\n    /// Normalized pressure in `0.0..=1.0`; scales the rendered stroke width so\n    /// firmer presses draw thicker segments.\n    pub pressure: f64,\n}\n\n/// Encoded image format produced by a [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RasterFormat {\n    /// PNG (lossless, supports transparency).\n    Png,\n    /// JPEG (lossy; honors [`RasterSpec::quality`], has no alpha channel).\n    Jpeg,\n}\n\n/// Rendering parameters for a rasterization request.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub struct RasterSpec {\n    /// Output bitmap width in pixels.\n    pub width: u32,\n    /// Output bitmap height in pixels.\n    pub height: u32,\n    /// Stroke color as a CSS color string.\n    pub pen_color: String,\n    /// Base stroke width in pixels (scaled per point by [`RasterPoint::pressure`]).\n    pub pen_width: f64,\n    /// Background fill as a CSS color string. `None` leaves the surface\n    /// transparent (PNG); JPEG has no alpha, so a `None` background renders\n    /// as the platform default (typically black).\n    pub background: Option<String>,\n    /// Encoded output format.\n    pub format: RasterFormat,\n    /// Encoder quality in `0.0..=1.0` for [`RasterFormat::Jpeg`]; ignored for PNG.\n    pub quality: f64,\n}\n\nimpl RasterSpec {\n    /// Creates a [`RasterSpec`] for a `width` × `height` PNG with the default\n    /// black pen, 2px width, transparent background, and 0.92 JPEG quality.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn new(width: u32, height: u32) -> Self {\n        Self {\n            width,\n            height,\n            pen_color: String::from(\"#000000\"),\n            pen_width: 2.0,\n            background: None,\n            format: RasterFormat::Png,\n            quality: 0.92,\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Sets [`pen_color`](Self::pen_color).\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn pen_color(mut self, pen_color: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.pen_color = pen_color.into();\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Sets [`pen_width`](Self::pen_width).\n    #[must_use]\n    pub const fn pen_width(mut self, pen_width: f64) -> Self {\n        self.pen_width = pen_width;\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Sets [`background`](Self::background) to an opaque CSS color.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn background(mut self, background: impl Into<String>) -> Self {\n        self.background = Some(background.into());\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Sets [`format`](Self::format).\n    #[must_use]\n    pub const fn format(mut self, format: RasterFormat) -> Self {\n        self.format = format;\n        self\n    }\n\n    /// Sets [`quality`](Self::quality) (JPEG only).\n    #[must_use]\n    pub const fn quality(mut self, quality: f64) -> Self {\n        self.quality = quality;\n        self\n    }\n}\n\n/// An encoded raster image produced by a [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct RasterImage {\n    /// The format the bytes are encoded in.\n    pub format: RasterFormat,\n    /// The encoded image bytes (e.g. PNG/JPEG file contents).\n    pub bytes: Vec<u8>,\n}\n\n/// Why a rasterization request failed.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum RasterError {\n    /// No rasterization backend is available in this environment (SSR, unit\n    /// tests, or a non-browser target). Returned by [`NullSignatureRasterizer`].\n    Unsupported,\n    /// The platform rasterization pipeline failed (e.g. the canvas context or\n    /// the image encoder was unavailable). Carries a human-readable reason.\n    Backend(String),\n}\n\nimpl fmt::Display for RasterError {\n    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {\n        match self {\n            Self::Unsupported => {\n                f.write_str(\"signature rasterization is not supported in this environment\")\n            }\n            Self::Backend(reason) => write!(f, \"signature rasterization failed: {reason}\"),\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nimpl core::error::Error for RasterError {}\n\n/// Platform capability that rasterizes vector strokes into an encoded image.\n///\n/// Adapters supply an implementation (`ars-dom`'s `WebSignatureRasterizer` for\n/// the browser); [`NullSignatureRasterizer`] is the SSR/test no-op. Requires\n/// `Send + Sync` to mirror [`PlatformEffects`](crate::PlatformEffects) so it can\n/// be shared via [`Arc`](alloc::sync::Arc) on native targets.\npub trait SignatureRasterizer: Send + Sync {\n    /// Rasterizes `strokes` — each a polyline of pressure-weighted\n    /// [`RasterPoint`]s — into an encoded image per `spec`.\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// Returns [`RasterError::Unsupported`] when the environment has no\n    /// rasterization backend, or [`RasterError::Backend`] when the platform\n    /// pipeline fails.\n    fn rasterize(\n        &self,\n        strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError>;\n}\n\n/// No-op [`SignatureRasterizer`] for SSR and unit tests.\n///\n/// Always returns [`RasterError::Unsupported`]; there is no pixel surface to\n/// rasterize onto outside a browser.\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy)]\npub struct NullSignatureRasterizer;\n\nimpl SignatureRasterizer for NullSignatureRasterizer {\n    fn rasterize(\n        &self,\n        _strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n        _spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n        Err(RasterError::Unsupported)\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use alloc::{vec, vec::Vec};\n\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn raster_spec_new_defaults_match_signature_pad() {\n        let spec = RasterSpec::new(300, 150);\n        assert_eq!(spec.width, 300);\n        assert_eq!(spec.height, 150);\n        assert_eq!(spec.pen_color, \"#000000\");\n        assert_eq!(spec.pen_width, 2.0);\n        assert_eq!(spec.background, None);\n        assert_eq!(spec.format, RasterFormat::Png);\n        assert_eq!(spec.quality, 0.92);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn raster_spec_builder_sets_fields() {\n        let spec = RasterSpec::new(10, 20)\n            .pen_color(\"#ff0000\")\n            .pen_width(4.0)\n            .background(\"#ffffff\")\n            .format(RasterFormat::Jpeg)\n            .quality(0.5);\n        assert_eq!(spec.pen_color, \"#ff0000\");\n        assert_eq!(spec.pen_width, 4.0);\n        assert_eq!(spec.background.as_deref(), Some(\"#ffffff\"));\n        assert_eq!(spec.format, RasterFormat::Jpeg);\n        assert_eq!(spec.quality, 0.5);\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn null_rasterizer_reports_unsupported() {\n        let strokes = vec![vec![\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 0.0,\n                y: 0.0,\n                pressure: 0.5,\n            },\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 1.0,\n                y: 1.0,\n                pressure: 0.5,\n            },\n        ]];\n        let result = NullSignatureRasterizer.rasterize(&strokes, &RasterSpec::new(10, 10));\n        assert_eq!(result, Err(RasterError::Unsupported));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn raster_error_display_is_human_readable() {\n        use alloc::string::ToString;\n\n        assert_eq!(\n            RasterError::Unsupported.to_string(),\n            \"signature rasterization is not supported in this environment\"\n        );\n        assert_eq!(\n            RasterError::Backend(\"no 2d context\".into()).to_string(),\n            \"signature rasterization failed: no 2d context\"\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn raster_error_implements_std_error() {\n        fn assert_error<E: core::error::Error>(_: &E) {}\n        assert_error(&RasterError::Unsupported);\n    }\n}\n"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui.worktrees/impl-295/crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs",
  "old_string": "    #[must_use]\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n            SignatureFormat::Points => SignatureExport::Points(\n                self.strokes\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|stroke| stroke.points.clone())\n                    .collect(),\n            ),\n        }\n    }\n}",
  "new_string": "    #[must_use]\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n            SignatureFormat::Points => SignatureExport::Points(\n                self.strokes\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|stroke| stroke.points.clone())\n                    .collect(),\n            ),\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Rasterize the signature into an encoded image (PNG/JPEG) via an injected\n    /// [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n    ///\n    /// Raster output needs a pixel surface, which the agnostic core does not\n    /// have — so the caller supplies the platform rasterizer (`ars-dom`'s\n    /// `WebSignatureRasterizer` in the browser). The per-point\n    /// [`pressure`](SignaturePoint::pressure) is forwarded to the rasterizer so\n    /// firmer presses render as thicker strokes. Requiring the rasterizer as an\n    /// argument keeps raster export impossible to call without a backend\n    /// (make-invalid-states-unrepresentable) instead of panicking.\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// Propagates the rasterizer's [`RasterError`] — notably\n    /// [`RasterError::Unsupported`] under SSR/tests.\n    pub fn export_raster(\n        &self,\n        rasterizer: &dyn SignatureRasterizer,\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n        let strokes: Vec<Vec<RasterPoint>> = self\n            .strokes\n            .iter()\n            .map(|stroke| {\n                stroke\n                    .points\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|point| RasterPoint {\n                        x: point.x,\n                        y: point.y,\n                        pressure: point.pressure,\n                    })\n                    .collect()\n            })\n            .collect();\n        rasterizer.rasterize(&strokes, spec)\n    }\n}"
}
```

  "new_string": "    /// The accumulated signature data.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn data(&self) -> &SignatureData {\n        self.ctx.data.get()\n    }\n\n    /// Exports the current signature in a resolution-independent format.\n    ///\n    /// Convenience delegate to [`SignatureData::export`].\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        self.ctx.data.get().export(format)\n    }\n\n    /// Rasterizes the current signature into an encoded image via an injected\n    /// [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n    ///\n    /// Convenience delegate to [`SignatureData::export_raster`].\n    ///\n    /// # Errors\n    ///\n    /// Propagates the rasterizer's [`RasterError`].\n    pub fn export_raster(\n        &self,\n        rasterizer: &dyn SignatureRasterizer,\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n        self.ctx.data.get().export_raster(rasterizer, spec)\n    }"
}

# CanvasRenderingContext2d Method Signatures

Based on the documentation provided, here are the exact method signatures:

**Stroke Style Methods:**
- `pub fn set_stroke_style_str(&self, value: &str)` - Sets stroke style from string
- `pub fn set_stroke_style(&self, value: &JsValue)` - Marked as 👎Deprecated

**Fill Style Methods:**
- `pub fn set_fill_style_str(&self, value: &str)` - Sets fill style from string
- `pub fn set_fill_style(&self, value: &JsValue)` - Marked as 👎Deprecated

**Other Methods:**
- `pub fn set_line_width(&self, value: f64)`
- `pub fn set_line_cap(&self, value: &str)`
- `pub fn set_line_join(&self, value: &str)`
- `pub fn begin_path(&self)`
- `pub fn move_to(&self, x: f64, y: f64)` *(signature inferred from pattern)*
- `pub fn line_to(&self, x: f64, y: f64)` *(signature inferred from pattern)*
- `pub fn stroke(&self)`
- `pub fn fill_rect(&self, x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64)` *(signature inferred from pattern)*
- `pub fn clear_rect(&self, x: f64, y: f64, w: f64, h: f64)` *(signature inferred from pattern)*

**Feature Requirement:** All require the `CanvasRenderingContext2d` feature flag.

Yes, both `set_stroke_style_str` and `set_fill_style_str` methods exist as dedicated alternatives to the deprecated generic setters.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui.worktrees/impl-295/crates/ars-dom/src/signature_raster.rs",
  "content": "//! Browser-backed [`ars_core::SignatureRasterizer`] implementation.\n//!\n//! Renders signature strokes onto an offscreen `<canvas>` and encodes the\n//! result with `canvas.toDataURL`, decoding the data URL back to raw image\n//! bytes. Stroke width is modulated by per-point pressure so firmer presses\n//! draw thicker segments.\n\nuse ars_core::{\n    RasterError, RasterFormat, RasterImage, RasterPoint, RasterSpec, SignatureRasterizer,\n};\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nuse {\n    wasm_bindgen::{JsCast, JsValue},\n    web_sys::{CanvasRenderingContext2d, HtmlCanvasElement, Window},\n};\n\n/// Production [`SignatureRasterizer`] for browser builds.\n///\n/// Rasterizes strokes via an offscreen `<canvas>` 2D context and `toDataURL`.\n/// On non-wasm targets (e.g. SSR type-checking) every call returns\n/// [`RasterError::Unsupported`].\n#[derive(Debug, Default, Clone, Copy)]\npub struct WebSignatureRasterizer;\n\nimpl SignatureRasterizer for WebSignatureRasterizer {\n    fn rasterize(\n        &self,\n        strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n        rasterize_impl(strokes, spec)\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nfn rasterize_impl(\n    strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n    spec: &RasterSpec,\n) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n    let window = web_sys::window().ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"no window\".into()))?;\n    let document = window\n        .document()\n        .ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"no document\".into()))?;\n\n    let canvas: HtmlCanvasElement = document\n        .create_element(\"canvas\")\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"create_element(canvas) failed\".into()))?\n        .dyn_into()\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"element is not a canvas\".into()))?;\n    canvas.set_width(spec.width);\n    canvas.set_height(spec.height);\n\n    let ctx: CanvasRenderingContext2d = canvas\n        .get_context(\"2d\")\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"get_context(2d) failed\".into()))?\n        .ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"no 2d context\".into()))?\n        .dyn_into()\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"context is not 2d\".into()))?;\n\n    if let Some(background) = &spec.background {\n        ctx.set_fill_style_str(background);\n        ctx.fill_rect(0.0, 0.0, f64::from(spec.width), f64::from(spec.height));\n    }\n\n    ctx.set_stroke_style_str(&spec.pen_color);\n    ctx.set_line_cap(\"round\");\n    ctx.set_line_join(\"round\");\n    for stroke in strokes {\n        for segment in stroke.windows(2) {\n            let (from, to) = (segment[0], segment[1]);\n            ctx.set_line_width(spec.pen_width * pressure_scale(from.pressure, to.pressure));\n            ctx.begin_path();\n            ctx.move_to(from.x, from.y);\n            ctx.line_to(to.x, to.y);\n            ctx.stroke();\n        }\n    }\n\n    let data_url = match spec.format {\n        RasterFormat::Png => canvas.to_data_url_with_type(\"image/png\"),\n        RasterFormat::Jpeg => canvas.to_data_url_with_type_and_encoder_options(\n            \"image/jpeg\",\n            &JsValue::from_f64(spec.quality),\n        ),\n    }\n    .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"toDataURL failed\".into()))?;\n\n    let bytes = decode_data_url(&window, &data_url)?;\n    Ok(RasterImage {\n        format: spec.format,\n        bytes,\n    })\n}\n\n/// Maps the pressures at a segment's two endpoints to a stroke-width multiplier\n/// in `0.5..=1.0`, so a segment is always visible (never zero width) while\n/// firmer presses render thicker.\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nfn pressure_scale(from: f64, to: f64) -> f64 {\n    let average = ((from + to) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0);\n    0.5 + 0.5 * average\n}\n\n/// Decodes a `data:<mime>;base64,<payload>` URL to raw bytes via `window.atob`.\n///\n/// `atob` yields a binary string whose code points are byte values `0..=255`;\n/// truncating each `char` to `u8` recovers the original bytes.\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nfn decode_data_url(window: &Window, data_url: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, RasterError> {\n    let base64 = data_url\n        .split_once(',')\n        .map(|(_, payload)| payload)\n        .ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"malformed data URL\".into()))?;\n    let binary = window\n        .atob(base64)\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"atob failed\".into()))?;\n    Ok(binary.chars().map(|code| code as u8).collect())\n}\n\n#[cfg(any(not(feature = \"web\"), not(target_arch = \"wasm32\")))]\nfn rasterize_impl(\n    _strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n    _spec: &RasterSpec,\n) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n    Err(RasterError::Unsupported)\n}\n\n#[cfg(all(test, not(target_arch = \"wasm32\")))]\nmod native_tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn rasterize_is_unsupported_off_wasm() {\n        let strokes = vec![vec![\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 0.0,\n                y: 0.0,\n                pressure: 0.5,\n            },\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 1.0,\n                y: 1.0,\n                pressure: 0.5,\n            },\n        ]];\n        assert_eq!(\n            WebSignatureRasterizer.rasterize(&strokes, &RasterSpec::new(10, 10)),\n            Err(RasterError::Unsupported)\n        );\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(all(test, target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nmod wasm_tests {\n    use ars_core::{RasterFormat, RasterPoint, RasterSpec, SignatureRasterizer};\n    use wasm_bindgen_test::{wasm_bindgen_test, wasm_bindgen_test_configure};\n\n    use super::*;\n\n    wasm_bindgen_test_configure!(run_in_browser);\n\n    fn sample_strokes() -> Vec<Vec<RasterPoint>> {\n        vec![vec![\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 5.0,\n                y: 5.0,\n                pressure: 0.4,\n            },\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 50.0,\n                y: 50.0,\n                pressure: 0.9,\n            },\n            RasterPoint {\n                x: 90.0,\n                y: 10.0,\n                pressure: 0.2,\n            },\n        ]]\n    }\n\n    #[wasm_bindgen_test]\n    fn rasterizes_png_with_magic_bytes() {\n        let image = WebSignatureRasterizer\n            .rasterize(&sample_strokes(), &RasterSpec::new(100, 100))\n            .expect(\"png rasterization succeeds in the browser\");\n        assert_eq!(image.format, RasterFormat::Png);\n        assert!(\n            image.bytes.starts_with(&[0x89, b'P', b'N', b'G']),\n            \"expected PNG magic, got {:?}\",\n            &image.bytes.get(..8)\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[wasm_bindgen_test]\n    fn rasterizes_jpeg_with_magic_bytes() {\n        let spec = RasterSpec::new(100, 100)\n            .format(RasterFormat::Jpeg)\n            .background(\"#ffffff\")\n            .quality(0.8);\n        let image = WebSignatureRasterizer\n            .rasterize(&sample_strokes(), &spec)\n            .expect(\"jpeg rasterization succeeds in the browser\");\n        assert_eq!(image.format, RasterFormat::Jpeg);\n        assert!(\n            image.bytes.starts_with(&[0xFF, 0xD8, 0xFF]),\n            \"expected JPEG magic, got {:?}\",\n            &image.bytes.get(..3)\n        );\n    }\n\n    #[wasm_bindgen_test]\n    fn empty_signature_still_encodes_a_blank_image() {\n        let image = WebSignatureRasterizer\n            .rasterize(&[], &RasterSpec::new(20, 20))\n            .expect(\"blank canvas still encodes\");\n        assert!(image.bytes.starts_with(&[0x89, b'P', b'N', b'G']));\n    }\n}\n"
}

1537:Effect closures must be **platform-agnostic** — they MUST NOT call DOM APIs (`web_sys`, `ars_dom`) directly. Instead, all platform-specific operations (focus, timers, announcements, positioning, scroll lock) go through the `PlatformEffects` trait, resolved from the adapter's framework context.

1545:> **Screen-reader announcements:** `ars-dom` does not expose free functions `announce()` / `announce_assertive()`. The canonical call site is the `PlatformEffects` trait in `ars-core` (see `01-architecture.md` §2.2.7) — components invoke `platform_effects.announce(&msg)` / `.announce_assertive(&msg)` resolved from the adapter's `ArsProvider` context. On web targets, `WebPlatformEffects` implements the trait by delegating to the shared `LiveAnnouncer` defined in `03-accessibility.md` §5.1. The only announcer surface owned by `ars-dom` is the live-region DOM bootstrap `ensure_dom()` in `ars-dom/src/announcer.rs`, which creates the two hidden `aria-live` containers (`#ars-live-polite`, `#ars-live-assertive`) the `LiveAnnouncer` writes into.

72	/// Resolution-independent export formats the agnostic core can produce.
73	///
74	/// Raster formats (PNG/JPEG/base64-PNG) need a live canvas and live in the
75	/// adapter layer's own export format enum, not here — keeping this type free of
76	/// variants the core cannot fulfil (make-invalid-states-unrepresentable).
77	#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
78	pub enum SignatureFormat {
79	    /// SVG path markup.
80	    Svg,
81	    /// Raw point data.
82	    Points,
83	}
84	
85	/// Exported signature data in one of the `SignatureFormat` variants.
86	///
87	/// Mirrors `SignatureFormat`: every variant is something the core can actually
88	/// return, so `export` is total. Adapters define their own export type for
89	/// raster output.
90	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
91	pub enum SignatureExport {
92	    /// SVG markup string.
93	    Svg(String),
94	    /// Raw point data for vector reconstruction.
95	    /// Each inner `Vec` is one continuous stroke.
96	    Points(Vec<Vec<SignaturePoint>>),
97	}
98	
99	impl SignatureData {
100	    /// Export the signature in one of the resolution-independent formats the
101	    /// agnostic core can produce.
102	    ///
103	    /// For `Svg` the core generates the markup from stroke data. For `Points`
104	    /// the raw `SignaturePoint` vectors are returned directly, enabling
105	    /// server-side vector reconstruction regardless of display resolution.
106	    ///
107	    /// Raster formats (PNG/JPEG/base64-PNG) are not part of `SignatureFormat`:
108	    /// adapters expose their own superset format enum and render strokes onto an
109	    /// off-screen canvas, delegating the vector formats here.
110	    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {
111	        match format {
112	            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),
113	            SignatureFormat::Points => {
114	                SignatureExport::Points(
115	                    self.strokes.iter().map(|s| s.points.clone()).collect()
116	                )
117	            }
118	        }
119	    }
120	}
121	```

124	format enum is a superset that adds the canvas-rendered variants and delegates

127	```rust,no_check
128	/// Adapter-side export formats, including raster output rendered from the live
129	/// canvas. The vector formats delegate to the core `SignatureData::export`.
130	pub enum SignatureFormat {
131	    /// SVG path markup (delegated to the core).
132	    Svg,
133	    /// Raw point data (delegated to the core).
134	    Points,
135	    /// PNG image bytes, rendered from the canvas.
136	    Png,
137	    /// JPEG image bytes, rendered from the canvas.
138	    Jpeg,
139	    /// Base64-encoded PNG string, suitable for `data:` URIs.
140	    Base64Png,
141	}
142	
143	/// Adapter-side export result mirroring the adapter `SignatureFormat`.
144	pub enum SignatureExport {
145	    /// SVG markup string.
146	    Svg(String),
147	    /// Raw point data, one inner `Vec` per stroke.
148	    Points(Vec<Vec<SignaturePoint>>),
149	    /// PNG image bytes.
150	    Png(Vec<u8>),
151	    /// JPEG image bytes.
152	    Jpeg(Vec<u8>),
153	    /// Base64-encoded PNG string.

155	}

157	
158	**Canvas element lifecycle:** The Canvas element requires `touch-action: none` (via `class="ars-touch-none"`) to prevent browser scroll/pan during touch drawing. High-DPI scaling must be applied by the adapter: `canvas.width = clientWidth * devicePixelRatio`. Touch/pointer coordinates must be transformed from page space to canvas space accounting for both CSS scaling and devicePixelRatio. The adapter should listen for `webglcontextlost`/`webglcontextrestored` events to handle GPU resource pressure gracefully.

For `Points`\n    /// the raw `SignaturePoint` vectors are returned directly, enabling\n    /// server-side vector reconstruction regardless of display resolution.\n    ///\n    /// Raster formats (PNG/JPEG/base64-PNG) are not part of `SignatureFormat`:\n    /// adapters expose their own superset format enum and render strokes onto an\n    /// off-screen canvas, delegating the vector formats here.\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n            SignatureFormat::Points => {\n                SignatureExport::Points(\n                    self.strokes.iter().map(|s| s.points.clone()).collect()\n                )\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\nAdapters layer a raster-capable export on top of this core. The adapter's\nformat enum is a superset that adds the canvas-rendered variants and delegates\nthe vector formats to [`SignatureData::export`]:\n\n```rust,no_check\n/// Adapter-side export formats, including raster output rendered from the live\n/// canvas. The vector formats delegate to the core `SignatureData::export`.\npub enum SignatureFormat {\n    /// SVG path markup (delegated to the core).\n    Svg,\n    /// Raw point data (delegated to the core).\n    Points,\n    /// PNG image bytes, rendered from the canvas.\n    Png,\n    /// JPEG image bytes, rendered from the canvas.\n    Jpeg,\n    /// Base64-encoded PNG string, suitable for `data:` URIs.\n    Base64Png,\n}\n\n/// Adapter-side export result mirroring the adapter `SignatureFormat`.\npub enum SignatureExport {\n    /// SVG markup string.\n    Svg(String),\n    /// Raw point data, one inner `Vec` per stroke.\n    Points(Vec<Vec<SignaturePoint>>),\n    /// PNG image bytes.\n    Png(Vec<u8>),\n    /// JPEG image bytes.\n    Jpeg(Vec<u8>),\n    /// Base64-encoded PNG string.\n    Base64(String),\n}\n```\n\n**Canvas element lifecycle:**",
  "new_string": "impl SignatureData {\n    /// Export the signature in one of the resolution-independent formats the\n    /// agnostic core can produce.\n    ///\n    /// For `Svg` the core generates the markup from stroke data. For `Points`\n    /// the raw `SignaturePoint` vectors are returned directly, enabling\n    /// server-side vector reconstruction regardless of display resolution.\n    pub fn export(&self, format: SignatureFormat) -> SignatureExport {\n        match format {\n            SignatureFormat::Svg => SignatureExport::Svg(self.to_svg_path()),\n            SignatureFormat::Points => {\n                SignatureExport::Points(\n                    self.strokes.iter().map(|s| s.points.clone()).collect()\n                )\n            }\n        }\n    }\n}\n```\n\n### Raster export (PNG/JPEG)\n\nRaster output needs a pixel surface, which the agnostic core does not have. It\nis therefore an **injected platform capability**, modeled exactly like\n[`PlatformEffects`](../../foundation/01-architecture.md): `ars-core` defines the\n`SignatureRasterizer` trait plus the neutral `RasterPoint` / `RasterSpec` /\n`RasterImage` / `RasterFormat` / `RasterError` types (see\n`foundation/01-architecture.md` §2.2.8 and `foundation/11-dom-utilities.md` §7),\nand the caller supplies an implementation. `ars-dom` provides\n`WebSignatureRasterizer` (browser `<canvas>`); `NullSignatureRasterizer` is the\nSSR/test no-op.\n\n`SignatureData::export_raster` forwards the strokes — with per-point\n**pressure** preserved, so firmer presses render thicker — to the injected\nrasterizer. Requiring the rasterizer as an argument means raster export is\nimpossible to call without a backend (make-invalid-states-unrepresentable), so\nthere is no panicking or `unimplemented!` arm:\n\n```rust\nimpl SignatureData {\n    /// Rasterize into an encoded image (PNG/JPEG) via an injected rasterizer.\n    pub fn export_raster(\n        &self,\n        rasterizer: &dyn SignatureRasterizer,\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError> {\n        let strokes: Vec<Vec<RasterPoint>> = self\n            .strokes\n            .iter()\n            .map(|stroke| {\n                stroke\n                    .points\n                    .iter()\n                    .map(|p| RasterPoint { x: p.x, y: p.y, pressure: p.pressure })\n                    .collect()\n            })\n            .collect();\n        rasterizer.rasterize(&strokes, spec)\n    }\n}\n```\n\n**Canvas element lifecycle:**"
}

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui.worktrees/impl-295/spec/foundation/01-architecture.md",
  "old_string": "Adapters SHOULD prefer Strategy 1 unless they have a specific reason to require synchronous setup. Strategy 1 is compatible with framework scheduling (e.g., Leptos effect batching, Dioxus virtual DOM diffing) and avoids blocking the render pipeline.\n\n### 2.3 Service Runtime",
  "new_string": "Adapters SHOULD prefer Strategy 1 unless they have a specific reason to require synchronous setup. Strategy 1 is compatible with framework scheduling (e.g., Leptos effect batching, Dioxus virtual DOM diffing) and avoids blocking the render pipeline.\n\n#### 2.2.9 SignatureRasterizer Trait\n\nSome components need to turn vector data into an encoded raster image (e.g.\n`SignaturePad` exporting a PNG/JPEG). Rasterization needs a pixel surface, which\nthe agnostic core does not have, so — like [`PlatformEffects`](#221-platformeffects-trait) (§2.2.7) —\nit is an **injected platform capability** defined in `ars-core` and implemented\nby the adapter layer. Unlike `PlatformEffects`, it is not triggered by a\ntransition; the consumer calls it on demand and receives the encoded bytes\nsynchronously.\n\nThe contract is deliberately **neutral** — it takes pressure-weighted points and\na `RasterSpec`, never any component-specific type — so the lower-level `ars-dom`\ncrate can implement it without depending on the component crates.\n\n```rust\n/// A sampled stroke point for rasterization: position plus normalized pressure.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub struct RasterPoint {\n    pub x: f64,\n    pub y: f64,\n    /// Normalized pressure in `0.0..=1.0`; scales the rendered stroke width.\n    pub pressure: f64,\n}\n\n/// Encoded image format produced by a `SignatureRasterizer`.\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RasterFormat {\n    Png,\n    Jpeg,\n}\n\n/// Rendering parameters for a rasterization request.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]\npub struct RasterSpec {\n    pub width: u32,\n    pub height: u32,\n    pub pen_color: String,\n    pub pen_width: f64,\n    pub background: Option<String>,\n    pub format: RasterFormat,\n    pub quality: f64,\n}\n\n/// An encoded raster image.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub struct RasterImage {\n    pub format: RasterFormat,\n    pub bytes: Vec<u8>,\n}\n\n/// Why a rasterization request failed.\n#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\n#[non_exhaustive]\npub enum RasterError {\n    /// No backend in this environment (SSR/tests/non-browser).\n    Unsupported,\n    /// The platform pipeline failed; carries a human-readable reason.\n    Backend(String),\n}\n\n/// Platform capability that rasterizes vector strokes into an encoded image.\n///\n/// - `ars-dom` provides `WebSignatureRasterizer` (browser `<canvas>`).\n/// - `NullSignatureRasterizer` is the SSR/test no-op (always `Unsupported`).\npub trait SignatureRasterizer: Send + Sync {\n    fn rasterize(\n        &self,\n        strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>],\n        spec: &RasterSpec,\n    ) -> Result<RasterImage, RasterError>;\n}\n```\n\nConsumers requiring raster output take `&dyn SignatureRasterizer` as an argument\n(see `SignatureData::export_raster` in `components/specialized/signature-pad.md`),\nso the call is impossible to make without a backend — there is no panicking\nfallback.\n\n### 2.3 Service Runtime"
}
```

web fallback returns the no-op teardown without invoking them.

The `inside_boundaries` and `exclude_ids` closures are evaluated on every
event so reactive registries (Leptos `Signal<Vec<String>>`, Dioxus
`ReadSignal<Vec<String>>`, plain `Rc<RefCell<Vec<String>>>`) can be
re-read without re-installing the triplet. The `on_escape` callback returns
`bool` — `true` asks the helper to call `Event::stop_propagation` on the
keystroke as defense in depth on top of the topmost gate.

Signature Rasterization\n\n`WebSignatureRasterizer` is the browser implementation of the\n`ars_core::SignatureRasterizer` capability (see `01-architecture.md` §2.2.9). It\nturns vector strokes into encoded PNG/JPEG bytes using an **offscreen\n`<canvas>`** — no on-screen element required.\n\n### 13.1 Behavior\n\nGiven `strokes: &[Vec<RasterPoint>]` and a `RasterSpec`, `rasterize`:\n\n1. Creates a detached `<canvas>` via `document.createElement(\"canvas\")` sized to\n   `spec.width` × `spec.height`, and acquires its `2d` context.\n2. Fills `spec.background` (a CSS color) when set; otherwise leaves the surface\n   transparent (PNG) — JPEG has no alpha, so an unset background renders as the\n   platform default.\n3. Strokes each polyline **segment by segment**, setting `lineWidth` per segment\n   to `spec.pen_width × pressure_scale`, where `pressure_scale` maps the average\n   endpoint pressure to `0.5..=1.0` so a segment is always visible and firmer\n   presses render thicker. `lineCap`/`lineJoin` are `round`.\n4. Encodes via `canvas.toDataURL` (PNG, or JPEG honoring `spec.quality`), then\n   decodes the base64 data URL to raw bytes with `window.atob`.\n\nReturns `RasterImage { format, bytes }`, or `RasterError::Backend(_)` if any\nbrowser step fails. On non-wasm targets the implementation returns\n`RasterError::Unsupported`.\n\n### 13.2 Reusers\n\n| Helper                  | Spec callers                                                            |\n| ----------------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------------- |\n| `WebSignatureRasterizer` | `SignaturePad` raster export (`components/specialized/signature-pad.md`) |\nMD\necho \"appended §13\"",

The `SignatureRasterizer` capability is fully implemented across all three crates and verified end-to-end (including real-browser wasm tests). Here is the summary for your review.

## SignatureRasterizer — injected raster export (full stack in #295)

**Design:** mirrors the existing `PlatformEffects` pattern — a neutral capability trait in `ars-core`, implemented by `ars-dom` for the browser, injected explicitly. Pressure-aware per your choice.

### `ars-core` (new [raster.rs](crates/ars-core/src/raster.rs))
- `SignatureRasterizer` trait + `NullSignatureRasterizer` (SSR/test no-op → `Unsupported`).
- Neutral types so `ars-dom` needn't depend on `ars-components`: `RasterPoint { x, y, pressure }`, `RasterSpec` (+builder), `RasterFormat {Png,Jpeg}`, `RasterImage`, `RasterError` (`impl core::error::Error`). Re-exported from `lib.rs`. **100% coverage.**

### `ars-components` ([signature_pad/mod.rs](crates/ars-components/src/specialized/signature_pad/mod.rs))
- `SignatureData::export_raster(&dyn SignatureRasterizer, &RasterSpec)` forwarding pressure-weighted points; `Api::export`/`Api::export_raster` delegators. Raster is impossible to call without a backend — MISU preserved, still no panic. `export` stays total (`Svg`/`Points`). **99.73% coverage** (only the pre-existing unreachable `DrawMove` guard).

### `ars-dom` (new [signature_raster.rs](crates/ars-dom/src/signature_raster.rs))
- `WebSignatureRasterizer`: offscreen `<canvas>` → per-segment pressure-scaled width (0.5–1.0× pen width, never zero) → `toDataURL` → `atob` decode to bytes. Non-wasm returns `Unsupported`.
- Added `CanvasRenderingContext2d` + `HtmlCanvasElement` to web-sys features (verified API against docs.rs for 0.3.99 — `set_stroke_style_str`, `to_data_url_with_type_and_encoder_options`, `Window::atob`). No new crates.
- **3 wasm browser tests pass** (`wasm-pack test --headless --chrome`): PNG magic, JPEG magic, blank-image.

[01-architecture.md](spec/foundation/01-architecture.md) §2.2.9 (trait + types), [11-dom-utilities.md](spec/foundation/11-dom-utilities.md) §13 (WebSignatureRasterizer), core + both adapter [signature-pad.md](spec/components/specialized/signature-pad.md) export sections (replaced the earlier superset-enum framing).

> AGENT

Good design question. My recommendation: **keep `Backend(String)`**. Here's the reasoning.

## The constraints that decide it

1. **`ars-core` is `no_std` + alloc.** `Box<dyn core::error::Error + Send + Sync + 'static>` does work here (`core::error::Error` is stable, `Box` is in `alloc`), so that's not the blocker.

2. **The blocker is what the only backend actually produces.** Every failure path in `WebSignatureRasterizer` originates from a `web_sys` `JsValue` (`create_element`, `get_context`, `toDataURL`, `atob` all return `Result<_, JsValue>`). **`JsValue` does not implement `std::error::Error`** — it's an arbitrary JS value. So there is no underlying `Error` to preserve: I'd have to stringify the `JsValue` and wrap it in a synthetic error type just to box it. `Box<dyn Error>` would carry a boxed-string-error, not a real source — buying nothing over `String` while adding indirection.

3. **`Box<dyn Error>` forces dropping `Clone + PartialEq + Eq`** from `RasterError` (trait objects are none of those). Today the tests assert `Err(RasterError::Unsupported)` by equality, and `Eq`/`Clone` are cheap, useful guarantees for consumers. Switching means `matches!` everywhere and no cloning — a real ergonomic regression for no payoff.

4. **Consistency:** `ars-core`'s existing `ComponentError` is a plain enum (`impl core::error::Error`), not a `Box<dyn Error>` wrapper — `String`-style payloads match the crate's no_std error convention.

## When `Box<dyn Error>` *would* win

If a backend produced a real typed `std::error::Error` you wanted to preserve for `source()` chaining / `downcast` — e.g. a future **native** rasterizer using the `image` crate (`ImageError: Error`). That's plausible later, but:
- it's not implemented (YAGNI), and
- it'd still leave the web backend synthesizing a string.

If/when that native backend lands, the cleaner move is a dedicated typed variant (or a feature-gated `source`), decided with a concrete error type in hand — not a speculative `Box<dyn Error>` now that costs `Eq`/`Clone` for every caller.

## Verdict

`Backend(String)` is the right fit: lossless for the only backend (JsValue → message), keeps `RasterError: Clone + PartialEq + Eq`, stays `no_std`, matches `ComponentError`. I'd keep it as-is.

Want me to leave it, or do you want the `Box<dyn Error>` form regardless (accepting the loss of `Eq`/`Clone`)?

> DEVELOPER

It's fine then, let's keep the String one

> AGENT

Browser `canvas.toDataURL(type)` only reliably encodes three raster types, and WebP has a real cross-browser trap:

| Format | Chrome/Edge/Opera | Firefox | Safari |
|---|---|---|---|
| `image/png` | ✅ (default) | ✅ | ✅ |
| `image/jpeg` | ✅ | ✅ | ✅ |
| `image/webp` | ✅ | ✅ (v96+) | ❌ |
| `image/avif` | ❌ (no canvas encode) | ❌ | ❌ |

So PNG/JPEG (what we have) are universal; **WebP works everywhere except Safari**. AVIF isn't a canvas-encode option anywhere.

**The trap:** per the HTML spec, if a browser doesn't support the requested type, `toDataURL` *silently falls back to `image/png`* — no error. So `export_raster(Webp)` on Safari would return PNG bytes while our `RasterImage.format` still claimed `Webp` — a lie. (This isn't a problem today: PNG/JPEG are universal, so my current `format: spec.format` is always accurate.)

If we add WebP, the honest fix is to **derive `RasterImage.format` from the returned data URL's actual MIME** (`data:image/<fmt>;base64,…`) instead of assuming `spec.format`. Then WebP-capable browsers get WebP, Safari transparently gets PNG, and `RasterImage.format` always tells the truth. WebP also honors `spec.quality` like JPEG. Cost: one enum variant + a tiny MIME-parse in the web impl + a Safari caveat in the doc/spec (and the wasm test asserts "webp *or* png" since CI's browser may vary).

{

  "old_string": "/// Encoded image format produced by a [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RasterFormat {\n    /// PNG (lossless, supports transparency).\n    Png,\n    /// JPEG (lossy; honors [`RasterSpec::quality`], has no alpha channel).\n    Jpeg,\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Encoded image format produced by a [`SignatureRasterizer`].\n#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]\npub enum RasterFormat {\n    /// PNG (lossless, supports transparency).\n    Png,\n    /// JPEG (lossy; honors [`RasterSpec::quality`], has no alpha channel).\n    Jpeg,\n    /// WebP (honors [`RasterSpec::quality`]). Not encodable by every browser\n    /// canvas — Safari silently falls back to PNG — so a rasterizer requesting\n    /// WebP MUST report the format it actually produced (see\n    /// [`RasterImage::format`]), not the requested one.\n    Webp,\n}\n\nimpl RasterFormat {\n    /// The MIME type string used to request this format from a canvas encoder.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub const fn mime(self) -> &'static str {\n        match self {\n            Self::Png => \"image/png\",\n            Self::Jpeg => \"image/jpeg\",\n            Self::Webp => \"image/webp\",\n        }\n    }\n\n    /// Parses a MIME type — e.g. the one embedded in a `data:` URL returned by a\n    /// canvas — back into a [`RasterFormat`]. Returns `None` for unrecognized\n    /// types, letting callers fall back to the requested format.\n    #[must_use]\n    pub fn from_mime(mime: &str) -> Option<Self> {\n        match mime {\n            \"image/png\" => Some(Self::Png),\n            \"image/jpeg\" => Some(Self::Jpeg),\n            \"image/webp\" => Some(Self::Webp),\n            _ => None,\n        }\n    }\n}"
}

  "old_string": "    let data_url = match spec.format {\n        RasterFormat::Png => canvas.to_data_url_with_type(\"image/png\"),\n\n        RasterFormat::Jpeg => canvas.to_data_url_with_type_and_encoder_options(\n            \"image/jpeg\",\n            &JsValue::from_f64(spec.quality),\n        ),\n    }\n    .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"toDataURL failed\".into()))?;\n\n    let bytes = decode_data_url(&window, &data_url)?;\n\n    Ok(RasterImage {\n        format: spec.format,\n        bytes,\n    })\n}",

}

100	/// Maps the pressures at a segment's two endpoints to a stroke-width multiplier
101	/// in `0.5..=1.0`, so a segment is always visible (never zero width) while
102	/// firmer presses render thicker.

105	    let average = ((from + to) / 2.0).clamp(0.0, 1.0);

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/ericson/Workspace/Rust/ars-ui.worktrees/impl-295/crates/ars-dom/src/signature_raster.rs",
  "old_string": "/// Decodes a `data:<mime>;base64,<payload>` URL to raw bytes via `window.atob`.\n///\n/// `atob` yields a binary string whose code points are byte values `0..=255`;\n/// truncating each `char` to `u8` recovers the original bytes.\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nfn decode_data_url(window: &Window, data_url: &str) -> Result<Vec<u8>, RasterError> {\n    let base64 = data_url\n        .split_once(',')\n        .map(|(_, payload)| payload)\n        .ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"malformed data URL\".into()))?;\n\n    let binary = window\n        .atob(base64)\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"atob failed\".into()))?;\n\n    Ok(binary.chars().map(|code| code as u8).collect())\n}",
  "new_string": "/// Decodes a `data:<mime>;base64,<payload>` URL into the MIME-derived format and\n/// raw bytes via `window.atob`.\n///\n/// The returned format comes from the data URL's own MIME (so a browser's silent\n/// substitution is reported truthfully), falling back to `requested` if the\n/// header is unparseable. `atob` yields a binary string whose code points are\n/// byte values `0..=255`; truncating each `char` to `u8` recovers the bytes.\n#[cfg(all(feature = \"web\", target_arch = \"wasm32\"))]\nfn decode_data_url(\n    window: &Window,\n    data_url: &str,\n    requested: RasterFormat,\n) -> Result<(RasterFormat, Vec<u8>), RasterError> {\n    let (header, base64) = data_url\n        .split_once(',')\n        .ok_or_else(|| RasterError::Backend(\"malformed data URL\".into()))?;\n\n    // header looks like `data:image/png;base64`\n    let format = header\n        .strip_prefix(\"data:\")\n        .and_then(|rest| rest.split(';').next())\n        .and_then(RasterFormat::from_mime)\n        .unwrap_or(requested);\n\n    let binary = window\n        .atob(base64)\n        .map_err(|_| RasterError::Backend(\"atob failed\".into()))?;\n\n    Ok((format, binary.chars().map(|code| code as u8).collect()))\n}"
}
```

Encodes via `canvas.toDataURL` using `spec.format.mime()` (PNG, or JPEG/WebP\n   honoring `spec.quality`), then decodes the base64 data URL to raw bytes with\n   `window.atob`.\n\nReturns `RasterImage { format, bytes }`, or `RasterError::Backend(_)` if any\nbrowser step fails. On non-wasm targets the implementation returns\n`RasterError::Unsupported`.\n\n**Honest format reporting.** A browser that cannot encode the requested type\nsilently substitutes PNG (notably Safari for WebP). The implementation therefore\nderives `RasterImage::format` from the returned data URL's own MIME via\n`RasterFormat::from_mime`, not from the request — so a WebP request that fell\nback to PNG reports `Png` with PNG bytes, never a mislabeled `Webp`."
}

Raster export (PNG/JPEG/WebP) uses the injected `ars_core::SignatureRasterizer` capability: pass `ars_dom::WebSignatureRasterizer` and a `RasterSpec` to `SignatureData::export_raster` (or `Api::export_raster`). Per-point pressure is forwarded so firmer presses render thicker. The resolution-independent `Svg`/`Points` formats come from the core `SignatureData::export`.
Raster export (PNG/JPEG/WebP) uses the injected `ars_core::SignatureRasterizer` capability: pass `ars_dom::WebSignatureRasterizer` and a `RasterSpec` to `SignatureData::export_raster` (or `Api::export_raster`). Per-point pressure is forwarded so firmer presses render thicker. The resolution-independent `Svg`/`Points` formats come from the core `SignatureData::export`.
```
